Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds. Near-
term event risks and high earnings expectations prompt us to trim equities to a 
Core holding. The US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain Overweight gold. Prices are likely to grind higher as near-term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead. In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran. These concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly for the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite the current challenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop. The US economic outlook remains well supported, and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA 
Chief Investment Officer, North Asia

### Page 4

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns  
 Fig. 2 Negative gold-USD correlation has held back 
gold amid USD strength. This is unlikely to persist 
Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long-term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and AxJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect them to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers. Our positive view on both markets’ 
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India. Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI-related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification. In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment-grade (IG) 
and high-yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3-7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance. We close our EU bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream, while rotating into alternatives 
helps reduce equity beta exposure. We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub-
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk. While we remain constructive on the long-term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near-term risk-reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long-term AI growth opportunities. The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI-related beneficiaries. For investors, the 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing 
AI concentration risk through a Total

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk, and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem. 
Non-AI-related factors, including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk. This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50% to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses, although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI-related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversification. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long-term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives, and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We maintain the probability at 30% as AI-driven investment growth continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027, as AI-led expansion continues. The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4%, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2.3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026, but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risks 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees core PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2.4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates at 4.0%, after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
ECB to hike once more in December, then pause. The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5% y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer-term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy, and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year. Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets, which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non-manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD 133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though, with technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first-home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local-government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a quarterly pace until Q2 2027. Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4%, and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five-year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3% in Q1 2027. Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle in H2 2027. Two government-appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

Global Market Outlook 11

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an Underweight view on Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data. With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3-7-year part of the US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yields to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond-supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda, 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where any signal of reduced long-end issuance 
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

7 Fixed Income – at a glance

Jonathan Liang, CFA 
CIO, Fixed Income & FX 
Cedric Lam 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI-driven supply is 
surpassing USD 250bn in 2026 and making up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for funding, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end of the yield curve and 
favour a duration of 3-7 years, where risk-adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 14 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe. The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY default rate 
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle. 
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics 
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 15 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post-pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could 
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks, as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.

### Page 13

Global Market Outlook 13 
Fig. 16 EM USD government bonds offer a yield pick-up 
of nearly 3ppt over DM peers 
Bloomberg EM USD Government Bond Index, yield to worst

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM LCY debt faces additional headwinds, especially as 
elevated US real yields and an appreciating USD dampen 
demand from foreign investors and reduce the comparative 
attractiveness of broad-based duration strategies. However, 
real yields remain comparatively supportive in several 
markets, and current valuations signal selective opportunities, 
especially given the restrictive monetary policy stances 
prevalent across many EM economies. Accordingly, EM LCY 
bonds retain their Core holding status, with portfolio emphasis 
placed on country selection and measured duration exposure, 
reflecting the differentiated currency dynamics at play. 
Key risks to both the USD and LCY segments include a 
potential escalation of the Middle East conflict, higher oil 
prices, renewed hawkishness from the Fed that could support 
further USD strength and weather-related disruptions, such as 
the effects of El Niño. Climate risks could prolong inflation 
pressures and hinder disinflation in vulnerable EM regions. 
Asia USD bonds – Core holding 
We retain a Neutral allocation to Asia USD bonds, an asset 
class that continues to reward investors with attractive 
nominal yields, favourable supply-demand technicals and 
robust credit fundamentals. Healthy corporate cash flows, low 
leverage and a high share of sovereign-linked issuers lend the 
segment a defensive quality relative to broader credit. 
The resilience has been evident year to date (YTD). Asia USD 
bonds have outperformed both DM government and DM IG 
corporate bonds on a total-return basis. We expect this 
relative strength to persist. The combination of carry and 
credit quality (over 60% of issues are sovereign or sovereign-
related) anchors our conviction in the asset class, even as we 
maintain a Neutral stance.

Bond opportunistic views 
Bullish US HY bonds 
We are opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. We expect 
HY corporate earnings and cash flows to remain solid and 
leverage to remain stable. US HY bonds’ shorter-duration 
profile offers lower sensitivity to interest-rate volatility, which 
is an attractive characteristic in an environment of elevated 
term premia. 
Bullish US inflation-protected bonds (TIPS) 
Real yields remain elevated versus historical standards, 
driven by rate hike expectations. Inflation concerns persist as 
energy prices remain subject to upside risks. We believe TIPS 
offer protection against near-term inflation uncertainty.   
Bullish AUD corporate bonds 
The threshold for further RBA hikes has risen, as higher 
energy prices weigh on the growth outlook and complicate the 
policy trade-off. The transmission channel matters too, where 
higher long-end yields could feed through to the property 
market, tightening financial conditions without the RBA having 
to act, casting further doubt on the case for additional hikes. 
Technicals have improved slightly as higher yields slow new 
supply. Supply remains a tail risk, though. The AUD bond

market is much smaller than the USD market, especially at the 
long end of the curve, where demand for duration is thinner 
and the term premium is most sensitive to supply. 
Bullish AAA-rated CLOs 
Concerns about spillovers from private credit are mounting 
amid deteriorating covenant protections in parts of the 
leveraged credit market. However, we believe AAA-rated 
CLOs backed by solid asset portfolios and substantial credit 
enhancements underneath senior tranches should help 
dampen credit risk and volatility. We see a compelling case 
for floating-rate and shorter-duration alternatives as portfolio 
diversifiers amid elevated term premia.  
Close utilities corporate hybrids 
AI capex is set to keep rising, but funding it has become 
harder. Tighter financial conditions now coincide with growing 
competition for capital. Utilities' credit fundamentals remain 
strong. Even so, we close our utilities corporate hybrids idea 
to lock in a gain of 0.5% as a risk-management step.

### Page 14

Global Market Outlook 14

Our view

We downgrade global equities from Overweight to a Core allocation as we expect higher volatility in the near term. We 
expect this volatility to arise as equity market leadership broadens during the transition to a mid-cycle bull market. Equity returns 
will likely moderate from the 15-20% annualised range of the last few years but remain positive. We remain Overweight US and 
AxJ equities, which are expected to deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 among the major regions. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, given structural AI-driven growth, and upgrade India to Overweight, for diversified 
non-AI growth exposure. South Korea is a Core holding, with attractive valuations but extreme volatility. China is also a Core 
holding, with attractive valuations but lacking a positive catalyst. We are Underweight ASEAN, which has a weaker growth profile. 
We maintain Japan as a Core holding as nominal growth supports broad-based earnings revisions. We also maintain a Core 
allocation to Europe ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings recovery. We remain Underweight UK equities, given the market’s 
limited exposure to growth sectors.

8 Equity – at a glance

Fook Hien Yap

### Page 15

Global Market Outlook 15

Shifting to a mid-cycle bull market  
The last few years have seen strong annual returns of 15-20% 
for global equities, consistent with the early cycle of a bull 
market, driven in large part by a rapid AI buildout. As the AI 
infrastructure buildout is increasingly debt funded – we 
estimate 40% of AI capex in 2027 would be debt-financed – 
the market is increasingly concerned about the quality of this 
growth and execution risks. We remain positive on the 
structural AI theme, but we expect investor focus to shift from 
rapid buildout to monetisation. During this transition, we 
expect market leadership to broaden beyond technology. 
We believe this shift will also be accompanied by higher near-
term volatility and a moderation in global equity returns, 
consistent with a transition to a mid-cycle bull market.  
Fig. 18 Projected 16% earnings growth for global 
equities in 2027 provides a strong tailwind for positive 
equity returns  
2026 and 2027 consensus earnings growth estimates for 
MSCI AC World sectors

Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have declined this year, with the 12-
month forward P/E falling to 19x from 22x at the start of the 
year. This reflects higher rates, a valuation headwind, and 
worries about the quality of growth, which is increasingly debt-
funded. As market leadership broadens and the US mid-term 
election nears, we expect higher near-term volatility in the US 
market. Nonetheless, US equities have broad-based strength, 
with the ongoing Q3 earnings season pointing to a robust 
economy. In a world of rising geopolitical risks, the US is also 
relatively insulated by its domestically driven growth. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ has the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key beneficiary of 
the AI capex cycle. AxJ’s P/E valuations have also dropped to 
attractive trough levels, the lowest among major regions. 
Within AxJ, we are Overweight Taiwan, which benefits from 
clear visibility on AI-driven growth and dominance in 
manufacturing advanced AI chips. We also upgrade India to  
Overweight due to its resilient domestically driven earnings, 
providing diversification beyond AI-related growth. India offers 
the highest exposure to defensive sectors (healthcare, 
consumer staples and utilities) within AxJ. Both Taiwan and 
India offer attractive returns on equity.  
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are positive 
on long-term agreements that improve earnings visibility for 
memory chipmakers. Valuations are attractive (the lowest in 
AxJ), with supportive corporate governance reforms. Still, 
extreme volatility remains the key overhang. China remains 
a Core holding, given inexpensive valuations, but re-rating 
potential appears delayed amid AI monetisation struggles, 
lingering regulatory risks and weak domestic demand. We 
remain Underweight ASEAN, as Indonesia’s downgrade 
warning, oil shocks and a well-supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 19 India offers highest AxJ exposure to defensive 
sectors (healthcare, consumer staples and utilities) 
Sector weights for MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal growth is supporting solid broad-based 
earnings revisions, with rising rates supportive of bank 
earnings. Corporate governance reforms also continue to 
boost shareholder value. The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings recovery after three 
stagnant years, and we expect to see a continuation of this 
narrative with Q3 results. Rising rates are supportive of banks’ 
earnings and fiscal expansion is benefiting domestic demand. 
The fiscal situation in France has no easy solution, but we do 
not expect a contagion widening in government bond spreads, 
as the ECB has tools in place to limit contagion.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK, 
making it lag the performance of other markets over the next 
12 months, although UK equities tend to hold up better during 
bouts of volatility.

Global Market Outlook 16

Add MSCI Switzerland and US Healthcare, 
remove Global Power & Electrification, MSCI 
Taiwan, Global Semis and US Comm Services 
We initiate an opportunistic Idea on MSCI Switzerland on 29 
October 2026, having initiated one on US Healthcare on 12 
October 2026. Healthcare and Consumer Staples account for 
over 50% of the MSCI Switzerland Index’s weight, providing 
exposure to more resilient demand. It also has a sizeable 
financial sector supported by steady wealth management 
growth and well-capitalised insurers, which benefit from 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We closed our opportunistic ideas in Global 
Semiconductors (9 September 2026 to 2 October 2026: 
+8.1%), MSCI Taiwan (9 July 2026 to 2 October 2026: 
+10.0%), US Communication Services (18 June 2026 to 12 
October 2026: +1.9%), and Global Power & Electrification 
(30 July 2026 to 29 October 2026: -6.5%). This reflects a 
tactical reduction in exposure to tech-related sectors amid 
higher expected near-term volatility, rather than a 
deterioration in the fundamental outlook. Over the longer term, 
we remain constructive on these structural themes and may 
revisit these ideas at more attractive entry points.

Ongoing Opportunistic Ideas 
US Healthcare: We expect US Healthcare to offer resilience 
amid shifting market leadership, with the sector projected to 
grow by 23% in 2027, the second-fastest growth rate among 
MSCI US sectors, underpinned by healthcare innovation. 
Euro area banks: A ‘higher-for-longer’ rate backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening 
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income is growing. Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Japan Banks: BoJ policy normalisation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and household wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
Global High Dividend: Provides a higher-income equity 
allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI ACWI 
and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We made two changes on 12 October 2026: we downgraded 
US Communication Services to Neutral to reduce 
concentration in tech-related sectors and upgraded US 
Healthcare to Overweight, where its defensive exposure 
stands to benefit as investors diversify beyond concentrated 
AI exposure. This month, we downgrade Europe ex-UK 
Industrials to Neutral, as energy-cost shocks and supply-
chain pressures outweigh fiscal support. Elsewhere, US 
Information Technology (IT) remains Overweight. Rising 
funding and execution risks warrant near-term caution, but 
structural AI tailwinds should continue to provide long-term 
growth opportunities. US Materials also stays Overweight 
on sustained demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials. We remain bullish on gold. The Financial sector 
remains Overweight across the US, Europe ex-UK and 
Japan, supported by stable earnings, elevated rates boosting 
net interest income and continued capital market recovery. 
In China, we maintain a broad Overweight stance to preserve 
sector diversification. IT and Communication Services 
remain Overweight, with rapid innovation and investment in 
AI localisation providing tailwinds. Beyond these, we are also 
Overweight Materials on structurally elevated commodity 
prices, Healthcare on biotech innovation and a robust drug 
pipeline and the Financial sector on easing margin pressure, 
recovering fee income and capital market reform.

Global Market Outlook 17

Our view

• We remain Overweight gold, but trim our three- and 12-month price targets to USD 4,350/oz and 4,700/oz, respectively. 
• We retain our three- and 12-month West Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil forecast at USD 100/bbl and USD 80/bbl, respectively.

Gold outlook: We remain constructive over 6-12 months, 
viewing the latest pullback as liquidity-driven rather than a 
deterioration in fundamentals. USD strength is a headwind, 
but gold’s sensitivity to real yields has eased amid continued 
central bank buying, led by China, and resilient investor 
demand. Speculative positioning near May lows raises scope 
for renewed inflows. High oil prices sustain near-term volatility 
but may eventually lift gold if they constrain global growth.

Oil outlook: Crude prices remain elevated as shipping 
disruptions drive freight costs to multi-year highs, sustaining a 
geopolitical risk premium. While Middle East exports have 
proven resilient, threats to production infrastructure are 
mounting. Refined products, especially diesel, face the 
greatest supply pressure amid depleting inventories and 
constrained refining capacity. With limited emergency supply 
buffers, oil markets remain vulnerable to prolonged disruption.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 18

Global Market Outlook 18

USD view

USD consolidation with limited upside: Our three-month US Dollar Index (DXY) forecast stands at 101, revised upwards from 
100.20 last month, reflecting a higher expected Fed rate path. With the October Fed rate pause and a potential December rate 
hike largely priced in, further tightening would offer limited additional USD support. Persistent inflation, elevated US government 
bond yields and resilient business activity continue to underpin the USD's interest-rate advantage. However, in September, 
payroll growth slowed sharply to 29,000 (from 133,000 in August), the unemployment rate rose to 4.2% from 4.1%, annual wage 
growth eased to 3.0% from 3.1% and housing starts fell 5.2% m/m. These signs of cooling demand temper expectations of more 
aggressive Fed tightening. We expect DXY to remain rangebound between 101 and 103, with our three-month forecast at 101.00. 
Peaking Fed rates and narrowing rate differentials favour gradual USD weakness: We maintain our 12-month DXY forecast 
at 98.50. We expect a Fed rate hike in December 2026 and another in H1 2027, taking the federal funds target range to a peak 
of 4.25-4.50%, before easing in H2 2027. Further BoJ normalisation and the ECB's relatively restrictive policy stance should 
progressively narrow the USD's rate advantage. Persistent fiscal and current account deficits increase reliance on foreign capital 
inflows, adding to medium-term pressure on the USD. Resilient US growth, AI-related capital inflows and elevated US 
government bond yields remain important offsets, supporting DXY’s gradual decline towards 98.50.

Rangebound with bearish bias: US-Canada trade uncertainty and softer 
Canadian employment limit near-term CAD gains. We expect the Fed to ease in H2 
2027, narrowing the US interest rate advantage and supporting gradual CAD 
appreciation.

USD/CHF 0.8250 0.82 0.80 
Modestly bearish: French fiscal uncertainty supports CHF safe-haven inflows, 
outweighing the wide US-Swiss rate differential, favouring appreciation towards 0.82 
near-term. Fading US rate support from H2 2027 favours further appreciation; zero 
SNB rates and CHF’s funding role are likely to keep CHF appreciation pace gradual.

USD/CNH 6.6690 6.62 6.52 
Modestly bearish: September exports grew 36.3% y/y and the trade surplus 
widened to USD 133.5bn, strengthening exporter USD conversion support. 
Industrial production growth accelerated to 5.5%, while retail sales growth remained 
subdued at 1.1%. PBoC fixing guidance and strong external flows support controlled 
CNH appreciation towards 6.52.

USD/SGD 1.2781 1.28 1.26 
Near-term rangebound; medium-term modestly bearish: The Monetary Authority 
of Singapore maintained its S$NEER appreciation policy unchanged in October, 
following a “very slight” increase in July. Q3 GDP expanded 5.4% y/y, supported by 
technology-related activity. The unchanged appreciating policy band and narrowing 
medium-term US rate advantage favour gradual SGD appreciation towards 1.26.

USD/INR 94.94 – 96 
Modestly constructive: October composite PMI strengthened to 56.7 from 55.9, 
while the RBI raised its repo rate by 25bps to 5.50%, supporting the INR through 
tighter monetary policy. However, September's USD 30.2bn trade deficit, elevated 
oil import costs and external funding needs sustain USD demand, favouring gradual 
INR depreciation towards 96.

USD/MYR 4.0857 – 4.00 
Modestly bearish: September exports grew 54.5% y/y and the trade surplus 
widened to MYR 33.7bn, strengthening export-related FX conversion. Resilient GDP 
growth of 5.3% y/y and a softer medium-term USD support gradual MYR 
appreciation towards 4.00.

USD/KRW 1,360 – 1,450 
Bullish: Strong semiconductor exports support Korea's external surplus, but 
concerns over AI investment returns and concentrated technology equity exposure 
have increased foreign portfolio selling. Overseas investment and the US-South 
Korea yield advantage sustain USD demand, supporting our forecast for USD/KRW 
to rise towards 1,450.

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
*As of 4am, 26 Oct 2026 SGT

### Page 20

Global Market Outlook 20

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) is at -2 (minimum: -5) in October, signalling a modest Underweight (UW) in global 
equities. Historically, a decline in the model score to -2 has been associated with flat to slightly negative returns. The shift was 
driven by stretched valuations in both global and Asian equities, weaker fundamentals and deteriorating market technicals. On 
fundamentals, market risk aversion has risen sharply, while PMI new orders have declined over the past three months, pointing 
to a slowdown in the pace of industrial expansion. On technicals, the share of equity markets trading above their 200-day moving 
averages, a key gauge of long-term support, has fallen below 60% for the first time since January 2025. The model rebalances 
in the middle of each month and has generated 11bps of alpha from the equity Underweight initiated on 15 October 2026, lifting 
YTD alpha vs. the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark to 4.0%.  
Our 1-3-month equity market regime model indicates contained bear market risks across US and global equities. 
Estimated bear market probabilities are 4.2% for the S&P500 and 7.7% for the MSCI AC World, compared with 0.9% and 6.4% 
respectively in the September outlook. Across both models, volatility measures are the main factors keeping probabilities low, 
supported by momentum signals. Together, these outweigh modest upward contributions from macro and cross-market factors, 
leaving little evidence of a bear market regime over the next 1-3 months.  
Our market diversity indicator flags reversal risk in MSCI Europe Real Estate and MSCI China Industrials, which are down 
over 12% and 19% respectively from their August peaks. US equity and USD positioning remain broadly neutral.

Global Market Outlook 21 
Fig. 33 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Modest equity underweight 25% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +1. Fundamentals weakened, with 
macro risk indicator rising above 0.5 threshold and 
the negative three-month change in PMI new orders 
pointing to a slowdown in industrial activity 
expansion 
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded the 
model’s 1 z-score threshold 
• Technical: -1. The percentage of equity markets 
above their 200-day moving averages has fallen 
steeply from above 80% since in August to current 
47% 
Estimated probability rose slightly from 21%  
• Conference board consumer confidence 
worsened to -14.3% y/y 
• PMI new orders to inventory ratio is at 1.14x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x 
• Conference board leading indicators improved 
but remained negative at -0.7% y/y 
• Fed funds rate to natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.7x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World MSCI Europe Real Estate and China Industrials 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. Bear market 
probabilities remain low at 4.2% and 7.7% 
respectively. Volatility measures are the main factors 
keeping these estimates low, supported by 
momentum signals and outweighing modest 
contributions from macro and cross-market factors 
• Market diversity indicator flags reversal 
risk in Europe Real Estate and China 
Industrials. The indices have declined by 
more than 12% and 19%, respectively, from 
their August peaks 
• Investor positioning remains broadly neutral 
across both US equities and the US dollar, 
indicating that investor exposure is not 
especially stretched in either direction 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time 
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses ML to forecast market regimes or future trends 
based on identified market drivers 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions

Global Market Outlook 26

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid-life wealth planning or fortifying assets for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals-
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well-diversified, long-term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short-term opportunities, and sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach 
Planning for Today 
Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

SC Wealth Select  
 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 27

Global Market Outlook 27

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi-asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer-term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

Advisory 
Process 
Following a 
holistic 
approach to 
managing  
your wealth 
We follow a rigorous process to ensure your 
needs and objectives are well-understood, and 
your portfolio is well-aligned and manages to  
deliver on these objectives. 
However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic rebalancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

重點摘要
1
分析師／林亞瑱
研究範疇／歐洲總經政策與市場分析
《景氣與獲利溫和成長支撐歐股，惟估值擴張受限》
2027年歐元區經濟預估溫和成長，西班牙與德國
表現優於義大利與法國。受通膨居高影響，預估歐
央2026年12月將再升息一碼，其後取決於通膨狀
況不排除2027年3月再升一碼的可能，惟二次通膨
效 應 有 限 ， 預 估 本 輪 升 息 政 策 利 率 高 點 將 落 在
2.75%~3.0%，其後轉為觀望。2027年道瓊歐洲
600企業獲利年增率預估放緩至高個位數。歐股有
望受惠資金在AI與非AI輪動下尋求多元配置需求，
輔以企業獲利成長，歐股行情仍可期，惟獲利增速
放緩及法國大選帶來的不確定性，將限制估值擴張，
歐股展望中立。
資料來源：高盛，2026/11，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11。

歐元區3Q26經濟維持韌性但強弱有別
《歐元區第三季經濟維持韌性，2027年經濟預估
溫和成長》
2026年第三季歐元區實質GDP季增0.4%，若扣除
愛爾蘭則季增0.2%，整體經濟仍維持韌性，惟主
要經濟體表現分歧。西班牙與德國經濟成長支撐
區域景氣，抵銷義大利成長停滯及法國經濟萎縮
的拖累。其中，法國今年以來受到出口及固定投
資疲弱影響，經濟承壓。
展望短期景氣，隨著消費者信心轉弱、大額消費
意願下降，冬季能源價格上漲的成本將陸續反映
在消費者端，進而侵蝕家庭實質購買力，加上借

貸成本偏高及融資條件趨緊，可能對冬季消費動
能構成壓力。

• 歐洲央行行長繼任人選，西
班牙央行前行長德科斯呼聲
高，預期既有政策路線大致
延續，對市場影響有限。
• 歐元區通膨居高，強化2026
年12月歐洲央行再次升息的
可能性。
《歐央行長繼任人選，前西班牙行長德科斯呼聲高》
歐洲央行行長拉加德將於2027年2月7日提前卸任，
歐盟理事會已啟動繼任人選遴選程序，目標於
2026年底前敲定接班人。目前市場呼聲最高的人
選包括西班牙央行前行長德科斯與荷蘭央行前行
長諾特，其中德科斯已獲德國支持，被視為最有
機會出線的人選。兩人在貨幣政策立場上有所差
異，諾特相對偏鷹，傾向及早採取行動抑制通膨
風險；德科斯則較為溫和，偏向循序漸進調整政
策。不過，無論最終由誰接掌歐洲央行，預期既

有政策路線大致延續，對市場影響有限。
《歐元區通膨居高，強化12月再次升息的可能性》
歐元區10月CPI年增率由前月的3.8%升至4%，能
源通膨年增率升至21%為驅動主因。能源成本上
升傳導至機票價格，推升核心CPI年增率由2.5%
升至2.6%。
2026年第四季整體通膨率平均預估將維持在4%以
上。歐洲正進入冬季供暖需求旺季，加上天然氣
庫存水位偏低，冬季天然氣價格預料將維持高檔，
並 可 能 進 一 步 推 升 2027 年 初 零 售 能 源 價 格 ，
1Q27通膨壓力仍高。由於歐元區通膨高於歐洲央
行9月基本情境預測，本行預估歐洲央行12月會議
將再升息一碼，將存款機制利率調升至2.75%。
2027年後續利率走向取決於能源價格及通膨發展，
不排除3月會議仍有再升息一碼的可能性。惟考量
能源價格上漲對其他經濟領域的傳導效應有限，
預估本輪升息政策利率高點將落在2.75%~3.0%，
其後央行將轉為觀望。
人選 西班牙 德科斯 荷蘭 諾特
背景
目前執掌國際清算銀
行(BIS)，曾任西班牙

行監理委員會主席

(FSB)主席
貨幣政策 溫和鴿派 較為鷹派

Document 495: 不做“屎山代码”制造机！“AI软件工厂”的“12条军规”

Source PDF: all report pdf/不做“屎山代码”制造机！“AI软件工厂”的“12条军规”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 写代码越来越快但质量却在下滑。新基准测试 Slop Code Bench 显⽰，⽬前最强模型在
持续演进代码库⽅⾯得分仅 16% 。 Factory 成员 Dex Horthy 提出 “ 软件⼯⼚ 12 条军规 ” ：⽤规
划、可视化、真实测试、代码规则和统⼀上下⽂约束智能体，并强调以⽤⼾结果和可维护
性衡量产出，⾃动化⼯具不能替代必要的⼈类判断。
摘要：
AI 写代码越来越快，但写出来的东西越来越难维护。
在近⽇举⾏的⼀场 AI ⼯程峰会上， Factory 公司成员、前 Human Layer 联合创始⼈ Dex Horthy 发
表演讲，抛出了⼀套针对 “AI 软件⼯⼚ ” 的 12 条核⼼法则，并联合多位业内⼈⼠正式发布 “ 反 Slop
宣⾔ ” 。
所谓 “Slop” ，是 AI 圈对低质量、⼤批量⽣成内容的俗称 —— 堆砌、冗余、看起来能跑但没⼈敢动
的代码。
“ 反 Slop 宣⾔ ” 的核⼼主张包括：“ 我们重视结果，⽽⾮产出；重视理解，⽽⾮速度。”“⼀群 Agent

在⼀天内产⽣的⼯作量，可能超过⼀个⼈⼀辈⼦能审查的总量。 ” 等等。
AI 写代码，最好成绩只有 16.3%
Horthy 在演讲中引⽤了⼀个刚刚发布的新基准测试：Slop Code Bench。
这个测试由威斯康星⼤学实验室研发，专⻔衡量 AI 模型在持续演进⼀个代码库⽅⾯的能⼒ —— 不
是⼀次性⽣成，⽽是像真实⼯程师那样，⼀个功能接⼀个功能地迭代。
结果并不乐观。 Horthy 说： “GPT 5.5 得了 15% ， Astra ⼤概是 16.3% 。我们在进步，但还有很⻓
的路要⾛。 ”
与此同时， Forrester 的研究数据显⽰，30% 的 PR （代码合并请求）在没有⼈⼯审查的情况下直
接合并，伴随⽽来的是更⻓的注释、更多的事故、更多的 Bug 。
Horthy 还点出了⼀个有趣的退化现象：现在的 Claude 和 GLM 正在⽤ Python 2 的⻛格写 Python 3
代码，⽐如重新使⽤%s这种⽼式字符串格式化⽅式，⽽不是更现代的 f-string 。 “ 感觉 Slop 在随时

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

间⾃我放⼤。 ” 他说。
“ 软件⼯⼚就是好的⼯程实践。 ”Horthy 称，关键不是简单增加并⾏运⾏的智能体数量，⽽是建⽴
⼀套能⾃动启动任务、持续测试、过滤低质量输出，并让团队保留关键判断的研发体系。
“ 你的标准软件⼯⼚⼤概⻓这样：有⼯程师、产品经理，也许还有 CEO 来定⽅向，他们把要做的
事放进任务追踪系统，有⼈去构建，跑单元测试，做 Pull Request ， CI 跑各种检查 …… 这些东西
30 年前就有了。 ”
他认为，让软件⼯⼚ “AI 化 ” ，本质上只需要⼀步：把 “ ⼈来构建 ” 换成 “Agent 来构建 ” 。其他环节
—— 规划、测试、审查、上线 —— 该怎么做还是怎么做。
“ 软件⼯⼚ ” 的 12 条核⼼法则
围绕如何避免 AI 提⾼产量的同时累积低质量代码， Horthy 在演讲中列出 12 项实践。其核⼼是：
让智能体获得清晰⽬标、真实反馈和规则约束，同时把⾼⻛险决策和质量责任留在团队⼿中。
第⼀条：平衡 “ 前向推⼒ ” 与 “ 反向制衡 ”
所谓 “ 前向推⼒ ” ，是让新任务⾃动进⼊研发流程。例如，将新的功能需求、线上告警或故障事件

直接转化为待处理任务，甚⾄⽣成初步的修复拉取请求（ PR ）。
“ 反向制衡 ” 则是指，代码在交由⼈⼯前，先经过智能体审查、⾃动化测试、持续集成和静态检查
等环节。 Horthy 称，⽬标是让智能体先修复明显、低成本的问题，尽可能提⾼交给⼈⼯时的初
始质量。
“ 当我凌晨三点被叫醒，我看到的应该是⼀个我们认为能解决问题的 PR ，⽽不只是⼀堆报错的堆
栈信息。 ”
第⼆条：认清模型的边界，⼀次性⽣成不等于⻓期维护
Horthy 认为，⽆⼈看管的模型未必会随着功能累积⽽⾃动改善代码库。
他表⽰，模型能够在短时间内完成相对封闭的任务，但软件团队更常⾯对的是连续需求变化。
每⼀次局部修改都有可能增加重复逻辑、削弱模块边界或加重技术债务。
因此，团队需要明确代码库当前状态、⽬标架构和可接受的⼯程规范，不能只将需求持续 “ 投喂 ”
给智能体。

### Page 3

第三条：先规划，再构建
Horthy 建议，是否投⼊时间写详细规格，不应只有 “ 详细 ” 或 “ 粗放 ” 两种选择，⽽应取决于两项因
素：未来发⽣变化的概率，以及变化发⽣后修改的难度。
“ 我不会在规划阶段和模型争论按钮该是什么颜⾊，但我会希望先把数据库 Schema 这类事情定
下来。 ” 他说。
换⾔之，数据库结构、核⼼接⼝、权限模型等后续修改成本⾼的内容，应在编码前充分对⻬；
⻚⾯样式、⼩范围实验或低⻛险功能，则可以采⽤更快的试错⽅式。
第四条：可视化对⻬
⽐起读⼀⼤堆纯⽂本，⽤ HTML 原型、图表、表格来对⻬意图效率更⾼。 “ ⼈类的⼤脑是视觉化
的。我们追了⼏百万年的猎物、摘了⼏百万年的⽔果，只读了五千年的⽂字。 ”
他提到，⾃⼰发布过名为 “Show Me” 的⼯具技能，⽤于将想法转为 HTML 原型、表格和图表，以
便团队更直观地确认意图。这项原则的重点在于，让⼈和智能体围绕可观察、可⽐较的结果沟
通，减少同⼀段⽂字被不同⼈理解为不同⽅案的情况。
第五条：给 Agent 提供真实的测试闭环

Horthy 提出，智能体需要看到⾃⼰⼯作的实际结果，包括截图、浏览器、视频、命令⾏输出，
以及可实际运⾏的开发或测试环境。
例如，在开发 iOS 应⽤时，不应只让智能体修改代码，还应让它能够完成构建、运⾏和测试。对
于⽹⻚应⽤，则可以让其通过浏览器查看⻚⾯并根据截图迭代。
他的说法是，要让智能体 “ 围绕⼀个明确的结果持续向上优化 ” ，⽽不只是根据代码⽂本猜测是否
完成任务。
第六条：⽤规则和⼯具对抗 Slop
针对 AI ⽣成代码中可能出现的冗余、复杂或不符合团队规范的内容， Horthy 建议将规则前置到⾃
动化⼯具中。
他在演讲中提到，团队可以使⽤代码质量规则集、 Lint 插件和语⾔层⾯的检查机制。例如，
Cloudflare ⾸席⼯程师  Dylan Mowat 维护的  Anti-Slop 代码库包含约 100 项规则； Factory 也提供
了相应的 ESLint 插件。 Slop Code Bench 中与 Python 代码质量有关的规则也可以被复⽤。

### Page 4

这些⼯具不能替代设计判断，但可以⾃动拦截⼀部分已知问题，减少⼈⼯审查被重复性问题占
⽤。
第七条： Slop 有时候是⼯具，不是罪
“ 不是每⼀⾏代码都必须完美。 ” 在原型验证、探索性实验，或者功能开关（ Feature Flag ）后⾯的
灰度测试中，快速⽣成代码是完全合理的。关键是它是否服务于最终⽬标。
第⼋条：持续复盘，积累复利
Horthy 建议，团队应对智能体会话、任务结果和评测数据进⾏持续沉淀，⽽⾮将每次使⽤ AI 都当
作孤⽴事件。
这包括记录哪些提⽰⽅式有效、哪些代码规则经常触发、哪些类型的任务容易失败，以及测试
中发现的典型问题。通过持续评测，团队可以逐步改进提⽰、⼯具链和⼯作流程。
第九条：统⼀决策与上下⽂归档
Horthy 提出 “ 统⼀ Forge” 的概念，即尽量减少重要上下⽂散落在代码仓库、聊天⼯具、⽂档平台
和个⼈对话记录中。
他提到，团队过去会通过 GitHub 等平台集中管理代码变更，但现在仍存在⼤量 AI 回答在 Slack 等
⼯具中被反复复制、粘贴的情况。这样的信息流转容易丢失 “ 为什么这样做 ” 的背景。

因此，代码、设计稿、关键决策和讨论记录应尽可能互相关联，便于后续成员和智能体理解项
⽬历史。
第⼗条：认清哪些东西买不来
在 Horthy 看来，虚拟机、开发环境、智能体运⾏框架和协作控制台等基础设施可以采购或使⽤
开源⽅案。
但与企业⾃⾝代码库⾼度相关的验证体系，很难完全交给通⽤产品解决。尤其是拥有数百名⼯
程师、多个代码仓库和既有⼯程规范的组织，只有团队⾃⾝最清楚哪些改动需要测试、哪些⻛
险不可接受。
“ 没有⼈能把你们的代码验证体系直接卖给你，因为只有你们知道如何测试⾃⼰的代码。 ” 他说。
第⼗⼀条：以提升⼈类杠杆率为核⼼
Horthy 表⽰，软件⼯⼚的⽬的应是提⾼⼈类时间和注意⼒的回报，⽽不是追求⽣成更多代码、
合并更多 PR ，或让更多智能体并⾏运⾏。
在他看来，衡量研发体系时，更重要的问题是：上线了什么、谁在使⽤、是否真正解决了⽤⼾
问题。代码⾏数、合并请求数量和智能体数量，都不能单独代表结果质量。
第⼗⼆条：持续校准⾃⼰和团队的质量底线
最后⼀条法则是，团队需要不断重新界定哪些输出可接受、哪些不可接受。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
27 收藏 分享到 :
随着模型、⼯具和⼯程流程快速变化，这条边界不会⼀成不变。 Horthy 表⽰， “ 没有唯⼀正确答
案 ” ，不同团队需要根据产品⻛险、⽤⼾影响和维护⽬标，确定相应的审查与验证强度。
“ 反 Slop 宣⾔ ” 正式发布
演讲最后， Horthy 宣布了⼀个联合项⽬：反 Slop 宣⾔（ Anti-Slop Manifesto ），联合发起⼈包
括 Swix 等多位⾏业⼈⼠，签署者来⾃ Zoom Info 、 xAI 、 OpenAI 等公司。
宣⾔的核⼼主张包括：
我们重视结果，⽽⾮产出；重视理解，⽽⾮速度。
⼀群 Agent 在⼀天内产⽣的⼯作量，可能超过⼀个⼈⼀辈⼦能审查的总量。
Slop 的成本是不对称的：⽣成⼀个千⾏ PR 或⼀份⼗⻚⽂档的成本趋近于零，但审查它的成本

依然相当固定。
AI ⽆法替代⼈类判断，它只能放⼤我们的判断 —— 有时候，也放⼤我们判断⼒的缺失。
Horthy 说： “ 我强烈相信，审查某件东西永远是有价值的。如果你什么都不审查，什么思考都不
带来，那你得到的就只是和其他所有⼈⼀样的 AI 输出。 ”
限免

Document 497: 不惜溢价46%！赛诺菲豪掷410亿美元收购美国生物科技公司

Source PDF: all report pdf/不惜溢价46%！赛诺菲豪掷410亿美元收购美国生物科技公司.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

赛诺菲以约 410 亿美元现⾦收购美国⽣物科技公司 Insmed ，溢价约 46% ，为三年来制药业
最⼤并购，旨在对冲核⼼药物 Dupixent ⾃ 2030 年代初起的专利悬崖。分析师认为交易 “ 争
取到时间 ” ，可将盈利增⻓延伸⾄ 2030 年代，但估值偏⾼。赛诺菲股价盘中⼀度涨 5.7% ，
Insmed 盘前涨 14% 。
摘要：
赛诺菲正以三年来制药⾏业规模最⼤的⼀笔并购，押注对冲核⼼药物专利悬崖的⻛险。
这家法国制药巨头周⼆宣布，以每股 175 美元现⾦收购美国⽣物科技公司 Insmed ，交易总价约
410 亿美元，较消息曝光前⼀个交易⽇的收盘价溢价约 46% 。赛诺菲⾸席执⾏官 Belén Garijo 称
此次收购是 " 加速增⻓的绝佳机会 " 。交易获两家公司董事会批准，预计于明年上半年完成。
这笔收购直接回应了赛诺菲最迫切的战略压⼒ —— 其头号畅销药、⽪肤与哮喘⽤药度普利尤单
抗（ Dupixent ）将于 2030 年代初起陆续失去专利保护。 Jefferies 分析师 Michael Leuchten 在研

报中写道，此次交易 " 争取到了时间 " ，有望将赛诺菲的盈利增⻓周期延伸⾄ 2030 年代，并为公
司 " 重建研发管线 " 创造空间。消息公布后，赛诺菲巴黎上市股价盘中⼀度上涨 5.7% ， Insmed 美
股盘前涨幅达 14% ，报 170 美元。
桥贷融资 350 亿美元，摩根⼤通与法巴平摊承销
据⼀位知情⼈⼠透露，此次交易由摩根⼤通和法国巴黎银⾏提供 350 亿美元过渡贷款融资，两家
银⾏共同承销，各⾃承担⼀半份额。赛诺菲⾸席财务官 Francois-Xavier Roger 表⽰，整体融资
⽅案结合了债务与⾃有现⾦，交易最早将于明年为公司经营利润做出贡献，并于 2028 年底前实
现每股收益增厚。
在财务顾问安排上，⾼盛担任 Insmed 独家财务顾问，摩根⼤通则担任赛诺菲独家财务顾问。
Garijo 最⼤⼿笔，直指呼吸与罕⻅病赛道
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
此次收购是 Garijo 今年出任⾸席执⾏官以来动作最⼤的⼀次战略部署。 Insmed 专注于呼吸道疾
病领域，旗下已有两款肺部疾病药物上市销售，并拥有更⼴泛的罕⻅病在研管线。
这也是赛诺菲⾃⼆⼗余年前由赛诺菲 - 圣泰拉波与安万特合并以来规模最⼤的并购交易，超过此
前收购健赞（ Genzyme ）、 Bioverativ 以及 Blueprint Medicines 等历次交易，同时是 2023 年辉瑞
收购 Seagen 以来全球制药⾏业最⼤的并购案。
Garijo 此前已明确公司的战略重⼼ —— 聚焦免疫学与炎症类疾病，以及虽患者群体较⼩但存在显
著未满⾜医疗需求、因⽽具备可观商业潜⼒的罕⻅病领域。在此次收购 Insmed 之前，她已推动
赛诺菲与再⽣元（ Regeneron Pharmaceuticals ）扩⼤合作，共同开发及商业化四款免疫系统疾

病相关新型抗体药物，同时也在主动精简研发管线，着⼒保留成功概率更⾼的在研项⽬。
战略逻辑清晰，但估值争议犹存
赛诺菲的股价⻓期以来是全球⼗⼤制药公司中估值最低的之⼀，落后于欧洲斯托克 600 医疗保健
指数， Dupixent 专利悬崖的隐忧是重要压制因素。
对于此次收购，市场解读不乏分歧。 KBC Securities 分析师 Simon Hox 认为，此举 " 战略层⾯积
极 " ，但价格 " 看起来偏⾼ " 。他在研报中写道： " 归根结底，赛诺菲买到的是额外的增⻓空间和时
间缓冲，⽽⾮ Dupixent 的完整替代⽅案。 "
Garijo 则表⽰，赛诺菲在完成此次⼤型收购后，仍有能⼒开展规模较⼩的早期阶段交易，以持续
丰富实验性药物组合。⾸席财务官 Roger 补充称，本次交易预计不会对赛诺菲 2026 年的业绩指
引产⽣任何影响。

Document 549: 凱基投顧_3665 貿聯-KY_李承泰_20261113

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_3665 貿聯-KY_李承泰_20261113.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 連接線組 ‧ 台灣

貿聯-KY 
(3665.TW/3665 TT)

營運執行力佳，3Q26 獲利率優於市場預期 
重要訊息 
貿聯-KY 公告 3Q26 EPS 21.76 元，優於市場共識 12%，主要受惠於費用管
控得宜帶動營益率優於市場預期 1.7ppts。因產品組合有利，毛利率 32.5%
亦略優於市場預期。3Q26 HPC 及半導體設備 (SPE) 營收分別季增 30%、
11%，營收比重 46%、13%，幅度優於其他業務的季增 10%。 
評論及分析 
Interplex 併表後更新。Interplex (星) 10 月營收 6,700 萬美元 (占合併單月
營收 18%)，對比 2Q25-1Q26 累計營收 3.92 億美元，顯示近期營收成長動
能強勁。管理層認為併購 Interplex 將成為公司提供更完整 AI 機櫃子系統的
關鍵一步，透過整合其精密機構件及組裝能力與貿聯既有 Power 與 Data 產
品，進一步提升價值主張，未來綜效亦有望延伸至 SPE 及醫療業務。凱基預
估 Interplex 將貢獻 2027 年營收 19%，獲利率則與台灣主要同業相近。 
上修 2026-28 年 EPS 預估 6%、5%及 2%。 2027-28 年獲利預估上修主要
反映 Interplex 併購案財務貢獻高於預期。若排除併購效益，考量 AI 資本支
出成長趨勢至少將延續至 2027 年，HPC 及 SPE 仍為未來數季主要成長動
能。貿聯主要美系 Hyperscaler 客戶預計於明年積極擴建 AI 資料中心，將同
步帶動 Power 及 AEC 產品需求。此外，光通訊子公司新富生 (中) 今年以來
單月營收已翻倍成長，有望自 2028 年起成為下一波主要成長動能。SPE 方
面，隨公司持續提供客戶更多 Box-build 產品，管理層有信心未來該業務成
長幅度將持續優於全球晶圓廠設備 (WFE) 市場。凱基預估 2025-28 年原
HPC 業務營收 CAGR 達 62%，2026-28 年 HPC 營收比重 (含 Interplex)達
49%、63%及 70%。預估 4Q26-1Q27 營收將分別季增 27%、3%，其中
Power 產品成長動能預期將優於 Data 相關產品。 
投資建議 
凱基持續看好貿聯在 HPC 市場之地位，維持「增加持股」評等，惟考量 AI
供應鏈面臨潛在逆風，下修 12 個月目標價至 2,580 元，係基於 2027 年 EPS
預估的 20 倍 (自 25 倍下調)，對比歷史區間 10-24 倍。 
投資風險  
景氣放緩；利潤率承壓。

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化
合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化
合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

### Page 1

十一月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 零售‧美國

Target Corp 
(TGT.O/TGT US)

轉型成果顯現 
重要訊息 
Target (目標百貨) 3Q26 每股盈餘 2.65 美元，優於預期，雖然部分受惠關稅
退款挹注，但同店銷售加速成長，顯示核心營運動能持續改善，也反映公司
提高食品與日用品等必需消費品、擴大商品選擇的策略已逐步見效。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 獲利超前 。3Q26 EPS 達 2.65 美元，年增 49%，優於市場共識
27%，主要受惠核心業務改善及關稅退款挹注。若排除關稅退款影響，EPS 
2.15 美元，仍優於市場預期的 2.08 美元。營收年增 7.1%至 271 億美元，
高於市場預期的 260 億美元。同店銷售成長加速至 5.6%，大幅優於市場預
期的 3.6%，且較上季的 3.8%提升，為本季最大亮點。來客數成長 5.0%，
主要由六大核心品類中的五項所帶動，而非少數特定產品貢獻，顯示成長基
礎廣泛。受惠供應鏈效率改善、去年促銷及關稅相關基期較低，以及高毛利
非商品收入佔比提升，毛利率排除關稅退款利益後仍年增 1.2 個百分點。 
轉型見效。我們認為 Target 轉型最重要的改變在於商品組合優化，持續提
高食品、日用品等高頻必需消費品佔比，帶動更穩定的客流及消費黏著度。
3Q26 食品與飲料品類銷售成長 7%，公司並新增超過 2,000 項食品 SKU，
較去年同期增加 40%，以提升顧客回購率。美妝業務 3Q26 銷售則成長超
過 9%，反映公司持續取得市佔率。透過必需消費品比重增加及擴充商品選
擇，Target 已逐步從過去較依賴非必需消費品的經營模式，轉向更具韌性且
可持續擴大市佔率的成長模式，我們認為將有助提升中長期競爭力。 
上調全年展望。受惠同店銷售表現超前及市佔率擴張，公司上調全年展望，
預估全年營收成長 6%，並將全年 EPS 調升至 10.65-11.40 美元，高於市場
共識。管理層指出，2026 年新開設 32 家門市，為 2009 年以來最高水準，
並成功拓展至 11 個新市場，因此 2027 年將進一步加大展店及門市改裝投
資，資本支出預計提升至 50-60 億美元。同時公司持續透過 AI 導入採購、
補貨及庫存管理，以提升營運效率及顧客體驗。我們認為效率提升及新店擴
張推動下，公司將維持穩健的營收及獲利成長動能。 
投資建議 
Target 同店銷售連續三季成長，反映轉型策略進入收成階段。隨著必需品佔
比提高、門市持續拓展以及獲利能力改善，我們看好評價提升空間。我們調
升評等至「增加持股」，根據 2027-2028 平均每股盈餘的 18 倍將目標價調
升至 195 美元。 
投資風險 
美國通膨壓力；庫存上升拖累利潤率。

資料來源：公司資料；Bloomberg；凱基

Target 3Q26 財報後電話會議摘要： 
 Target 上調 2026 年全年展望，預估營收成長 6%，營業利益率約
6.2%，EPS 為 10.65-11.40 美元。管理層表示，目前食品、美妝及其他
主要品類持續帶動客流成長，新店及改裝店效益逐步顯現，公司對未來
營運發展維持正面看法。 
 3Q26 營收年增 7.1%，同店銷售成長 5.6%，其中來客數成長 5%。管
理層表示，同店銷售成長主要來自客流增加，而非價格因素。第三季成
長橫跨多數核心品類，且 7 月至 9 月皆維持穩健表現。10 月銷售略有放
緩，但 11 月再度加速，整體表現符合公司預期。 
 食品與飲料銷售年增接近 7%，為帶動客流的重要來源。零食、糖果及
飲料表現尤其突出。管理層指出，今年以來含食品商品的交易筆數較去
年增加約 4,000 萬筆，顯示食品品類持續提升消費者到店頻率。 
 美妝業務第三季成長超過 9%。護膚、香氛、護髮及美甲等品類均維持
高個位數至雙位數成長。公司表示新推出的 Target Beauty Studio 表現
優於預期，目前已拓展至超過 600 家門市。 
 Fun 101 品類第三季成長 27%。部分成長來自集換式卡牌需求，但玩
具、運動用品、電子配件及流行文化商品亦全面成長。 
 3Q26 毛利率為 30.5%，較去年同期提升 230 個基點。本季公司認列約
3 億美元關稅退稅利益，約貢獻 0.5 美元 EPS。排除退稅影響，第三季
營業利益仍成長 24%。 
 公司已新開 32 家門市，為 2009 年以來最多，並完成超過 130 家店面
改裝。此外，公司進入 11 個新市場，新增約 200 萬名潛在消費者。 
 2026 年資本支出約 50 億美元，並規劃 2027 年提升至 50-60 億美元，
主要投入新店、店面改裝、供應鏈及科技建設。 
 公司已與 ChatGPT、Gemini 及 Copilot 等平台合作，布局 AI 購物與搜
尋應用。自有 AI 搜尋工具 Ask Target 目前測試結果正面，使用該工具
的消費者轉換率約為一般搜尋方式的兩倍。

美國 Target Corp

十一月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 4

圖 6：公司概況  圖 7：美容與家庭必需品營收占比最高 
成立於 1902 年，目標百貨 (Target) 為美國大型綜合零售商，
販售商品多元包括食品飲料、家居用品、服裝及電子產品等。
目前在美國共有 2,019 家門市，公司也積極發展電商業務，提
供網上購物、店內取貨和送貨到家的服務。2025 年實體店面
銷售佔比為 79%，電商佔比則為 21%。

### Page 6

美國 Target Corp

十一月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

期中選舉後先跌後漲，美股長期趨勢向上

期中選舉後，美股先是大幅下挫後出現反彈，目前科技相關類股及指數估值已大
幅修正至「便宜」位階，建議投資人可逢低布局或維持定期定額買進，享受全球
經濟長期成長紅利。 
出刊日：2026/11/20 
277 期

### Page 2

2026

02-2771-6699 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
動見觀瞻

本期內容： 
1. 3Q 財報大致呈現
正向 
2. 科技類股估值已
大幅修正 
3. 美股長期趨勢向
上

重點摘要 
■ 美國企業在經濟表現強勁下，3Q 企業
財報依舊維持過半優於市場預期的好
表現。其中，資訊科技及能源仍是全年
表現最佳產業。

■ 近期股市並未因為財報持續報喜而樂
觀，反而因為民主黨對 AI 相對嚴厲的
監管態度而擔憂並再次下修科技股估
值，使科技相關類股及指數估值都已
來到相對「便宜」位階。

■ 儘管歷史上出現多次股市大幅下挫的
極端事件，但在經濟持續成長帶動下，
美股長期維持上漲趨勢，建議投資人
應逢低布局或維持定期定額買進，方
能享受全球經濟長期成長紅利。

標普 500 指數修正後快速反彈

資料來源：Bloomberg, 2026/11/13

期中選舉後先跌後漲，美股長期趨勢向上

### Page 3

2026

02-2771-6699 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號

▋ 3Q 財報大致呈現正向 
時間來到 11 月中旬，美國 3Q 企業財報季
即將迎來尾聲，彭博統計數據顯示，標普
500 企業獲利依舊維持穩健，營收/盈餘也
過半都優於市場預期，顯示大企業持續受惠
美國經濟景氣強韌。

圖一: 標普 500 企業財報普遍優於預期

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/11/13

然而，產業表現逐步出現分化，資訊科技產
業受惠 AI 發展，在企業營收與盈餘兩部分
都有超過 75%企業優於市場預期，且年增
率更是僅次能源產業達 34%/82%，展現驚
人的成長動能。但金融、必需消費、醫療保
健等產業則相對遜色，僅有約 50~60%企
業表現優於預期，且年增率僅維持中等個位
數成長。顯示近期市場投資重心高度集中科
技產業及 AI 發展，儘管帶動美國經濟動能
維持強勁，但也無法同步擴於全產業發展，
仍會有強弱之分，而企業財報就是投資人驗
證觀點的最佳時機。 
▋ 科技類股估值已大幅修正 
財報數據強勁並不代表股價就會強勢上漲，
尤其是民主黨在期中選舉大勝後，市場擔憂
其對資料中心建設態度相對嚴厲且要求增
加監管措施的政見會對 AI 發展造成阻礙，
使目前市場預期的發展進度及獲利預期將
再次延後，對相關企業股票估值形成負向效
應。因此，儘管科技股自 10 月初美股在創
歷史新高後就持續修正，仍在 11 月初的選
舉結束後放大跌幅並重挫。所幸，隨市場走
出恐慌性賣壓並恢復理性後，科技股迎來小
幅漲勢，暫時讓賣壓未再繼續擴張。

圖二: 科技相關指數已經大幅修正

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/11/13

以估值而言，科技相關指數(那斯達克 100
指數、費城半導體指數)之未來 12 個月預期
本益比皆低於過去 5 年平均水準，仍處相
對「便宜」區間，投資人應把握機會逢低買
進，降低長期持有成本。標普 500 指數雖
有其他產業支撐表現，仍在科技巨頭影響下，
估值也重回 5 年平均下方，惟修正比例遠
遠低於上述之科技相關指數。而道瓊及羅素
2000 指數目前估值仍高於 5 年平均，此時
並非最佳進場布局時機。

台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號

▋ 美股長期趨勢向上 
儘管近期股市呈現橫向大幅震盪，甚至出現
向下修正態勢，使部分投資人擔心自 2023
年以來的 AI 趨勢已形成泡沫且即將破裂。
然而，在企業獲利展望未見轉向，且經濟數
據持續維持火熱下，無法斷定「AI 是否已走
向泡沫」，因股價上漲動能來自企業獲利，
而企業獲利也實質反應至各國經濟數據，並
非「空買空賣」或是幻想，而是實實在在的
資本投入及產出。而過去每半年就會出現一
次事件使市場修正科技股估值，也足以代表
市場對 AI 泡沫議題仍具有一定的警戒，每
一次的股市修正便是市場阻止泡沫出現異
常增生或是降低泡沫的動作。

圖三: 美股長期維持上行趨勢

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/11/3 
圖四: 歷史經驗顯示，民主黨大勝表現更佳

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/11/3 
以歷史經驗來說，美股長期趨勢向上，主要
反應全球 GDP 仍是逐年持續成長，而只要
經濟持續成長，股市自然就是維持上漲。回
顧自 1930 年期中選舉以來的美股標普 500
指數表現，期中選舉後 1 個月、3 個月與 6
個月之美股表現，標普 500 指數在不同區
間皆維持正報酬，且持有期間越長，上漲幅
度越明顯。顯示期中選舉後政治不確定性將
持續降低，加上資金重回經濟與企業獲利前
景基本盤，美股將重回長期上行格局。

▋ 結論 
美國企業在經濟表現強勁下，3Q 企業財報
依舊維持過半優於市場預期的好表現，然而，
近期股市並未因為財報持續報喜而樂觀，反
而因民主黨對 AI 相對嚴厲的監管態度而擔
憂並再次下修科技股估值。因此，儘管美股
自 10 月以來便持續修正，11 月初仍再次
大幅下殺，使科技相關類股及指數估值都已
來到相對「便宜」位階。同時，儘管歷史上
出現多次股市大幅下挫的極端事件，但在經
濟持續成長帶動下，美股長期維持上漲趨勢，
建議投資人可逢低布局或維持定期定額買
進，享受全球經濟長期成長紅利。

观点综述
核心结论：国内 10 月工业增加值同比回落至4.8%、社会消费品零售总额仅增0.4%、 1-10 月固定资产投资同比-8.1%， 11 月 LPR 连续第 18 个月持
稳， A 股缩量走弱、上证周跌1.56%；海外美联储 10 月会议纪要显示 “ 几乎所有 ” 官员认为未来数月仍需进一步加息， 12 月加息 25 个基点概率
96.4%，美债 10 年期回落至 5.04% 但已连续 29 个交易日高于 5% ，标普 500 六连跌、单周-5.10%，纳指-5.00%，伦敦金暴跌4.66%至 3709.50 美

元创 14 个月新低， WTI 原油跌6.04%至 83.21 美元。
主要论点：
1. 国内需求： 10 月工增4.8%、社零0.4%、固投-8.1%， 11 月 LPR 持稳，政策进入观察期。
2. 美国紧缩： 10 月纪要 “ 几乎全体 ” 支持再加息， 12 月加息 25 个基点概率96.4%， 2 年期美债回落至4.76%。
3. 资产重估：标普周跌5.10%，伦敦金跌4.66%至 3709.50 美元， WTI 跌6.04%。
风险提示： 12 月一次性加息 50 个基点的可能性； AI 资本开支回报与相关债务风险；黄金流动性抛售延续；国内地产与消费修复偏慢、 PPI 向 CPI 传导有限。
行情回顾

Document 706: 米住宅着工・許可件数（26年10月）－着工件数は130.9万件と前月（119.1万件）および市場予想（125.0万件）を上回る

Source PDF: all report pdf/米住宅着工・許可件数（26年10月）－着工件数は130.9万件と前月（119.1万件）および市場予想（125.0万件）を上回る.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

1． 結果の概要：住宅着工件数は市場予想を上回った一方、建設許可件数は市場予想を下回る 
11月18日、米国センサス局は 26年10月の住宅着工、許可件数を発表した。住宅着工件数（季
節調整済、年率）は 130.9万件（前月改定値：119.1万件）と 122.1万件から上方修正された前月
値および市場予想の 125.0万件（Bloomberg集計の中央値）を上回った（図表 1、図表 3）。 
先行指標である建設許可件数（季節調整済、年率）は 129.5万件（前月：139.8万件）とこちら
は前月から低下し、市場予想の 137.5万件も下回った（図表 2、図表 5）。

2． 結果の評価：建設許可件数や住宅市場指数が悪化、住宅市場の低迷が続く 
住宅着工件数の伸びは前月比+9.9％（前月：▲7.0％）と4ヵ月ぶりにプラスに転じた（図表 3）。
戸建て住宅が+4.0％（前月：▲5.8％）と前月からプラスに転じたほか、2戸以上を合計した集合住
宅が+23.6％（前月：▲9.5％）と 4カ月ぶりに大幅なプラスに転じ着工件数を押し上げた（図表 4）。  
前年同月比は+2.8％（前月：▲9.7％）と 4カ月ぶりにプラスに転じた。戸建てが▲3.2％（前月：
▲0.7％）と2ヵ月連続でマイナスとなった一方、集合住宅+17.1％（前月：▲25.3％）と前月から
大幅なプラスに転じて着工件数全体を押し上げた。 
地域別寄与度（前月比）は、西部が▲0.5％ポイント（前月：▲6.1％ポイント）と 2ヵ月連続の
マイナスとなったほか、北東部が▲2.1％ポイント（前月：+0.7％ポイント）と前月からマイナスに
転じた。一方、南部が+6.0％ポイント（前月：+0.2％ポイント）と 2ヵ月連続のプラスとなったほ
か、中西部が+6.5％ポイント（前月：▲1.8％ポイント）と前月からプラスに転じるなどまちまちの
結果となった。

30（前月：32）、客足が 16（前月：18）と前月から
それぞれ▲2ポイント低下した。

関税によって高騰する建築資材費、建設労働者の慢性的な不足、住宅価格の手頃さを損なう過剰な
規制など、供給面で様々な課題に直面している」と指摘したほか、「 7％を超える住宅ローン金利、
消費者信頼感の低下、そして不確実な経済見通しが、買い手の購買意欲を阻害している」として、
住宅市場が供給と需要両面の問題を抱えている状況を示した。

Document 710: 米国コアCPIが3カ月連続で予想を上振れ(2026年10月) ～12月FOMCでの25bpの利上げが濃厚、一部で50bpの利上げ警戒～
